          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Stage 2: Exposure estimation.} Per-item exposure $e_i$ is estimated from external evidence independent of the response data used in Stages~1 and~3. The primary signal is \\emph{reference model anchoring}: using a fixed reference model (e.g., gpt-4-0314) as anchor, we compute win-rate residuals for each target model against difficulty-adjusted expectations; items where target models systematically outperform the anchor beyond what difficulty predicts are flagged as potentially exposed. The secondary signal is \\emph{cross-model agreement}: items receiving uniformly high scores across architecturally diverse models suggest exposure rather than genuine easiness, quantified via $z$-score standardization of the inter-model agreement distribution. These signals are combined as a weighted composite (75\\% anchoring, 25\\% agreement), clipped to $[0, 1]$, and treated as fixed in Stage~3. Where model log-probabilities are accessible, LogProber can provide an additional gray-box memorization signal, though it is not used in our current experiments.",
          " ",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Judge Bias in LLM Evaluation.}\nCyclicJudge \\citep{cyclicjudge2026} eliminates average bias via round-robin assignment but treats residual variation as genuine ability. UDA \\citep{uda2026} learns a neural K-factor reducing inter-judge deviation by 63.4\\% without modeling contamination. \\citet{fiedler2026bias_uncertainty} formalize judge variability via $J/\\Delta J$ diagnostics; \\citet{ye2024justice} and \\citet{panickssery2024selffavor} document position, verbosity, and self-favoritism biases. All model only the judge facet, absorbing contamination into abilities (Section~\\ref{sec:confounding}).\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination Detection and Adjustment.}\nSeveral methods detect or adjust for benchmark contamination---ConStat \\citep{constat2024}, DCR \\citep{dcr2025}, LogProber \\citep{logprober2024}, Min-K\\% \\citep{mink2024}---but all assume a fixed rater, so judge severity can masquerade as contamination. \\citet{preference_leakage2026} identify judge-side contamination (same-family leakage), complementary to G-MFRM's examinee-side focus.\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination Avoidance.}\nLiveBench \\citep{livebench2025}, TRUCE \\citep{truce2024}, and tinyBenchmarks \\citep{polo2024tinybenchmarks} prevent or reduce contamination by design; G-MFRM corrects for residual contamination in existing benchmarks.\n\n\\paragraph{IRT and MFRM for LLM Evaluation.}\n\\citet{jiao2025mfrm_llm} apply MFRM to LLM raters without contamination terms; \\citet{uto2020generalized}, \\citet{choi2026irt_judge}, and PSN-IRT \\citep{zhou2025psnirt} extend IRT/MFRM along other axes but none include contamination facets. The closest antecedent, DG-IRT \\citep{shu2013dgirt}, is compared in Section~\\ref{sec:model_spec}.",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 4,
        "oldLines": 11,
        "newStart": 4,
        "newLines": 8,
        "lines": [
          " \\paragraph{Judge Bias in LLM Evaluation.}",
          " CyclicJudge \\citep{cyclicjudge2026} eliminates average bias via round-robin assignment but treats residual variation as genuine ability. UDA \\citep{uda2026} learns a neural K-factor reducing inter-judge deviation by 63.4\\% without modeling contamination. \\citet{fiedler2026bias_uncertainty} formalize judge variability via $J/\\Delta J$ diagnostics; \\citet{ye2024justice} and \\citet{panickssery2024selffavor} document position, verbosity, and self-favoritism biases. All model only the judge facet, absorbing contamination into abilities (Section~\\ref{sec:confounding}).",
          " ",

          "+\\paragraph{Contamination Detection and Avoidance.}",

LiveBench \\citep{livebench2025}, TRUCE \\citep{truce2024}, and tinyBenchmarks \\citep{polo2024tinybenchmarks} prevent contamination by design; G-MFRM corrects for residual contamination in existing benchmarks.",

Let me try condensing the IRT paragraph - I can combine the citations about MFRM and IRT extensions into a more compact statement without losing the key point about how prior work extends these frameworks. Looking at the error propagation results, the table shows a clear threshold effect: below σ = 0.2, the errors are modest at 5.9% for ability and 7.9% for contamination, but once we cross σ = 0.5, both metrics degrade sharply to 36.9% and 57.9% respectively. Contamination estimates are particularly vulnerable because they only appear in the likelihood through their interaction with the error term. Despite this sensitivity, the rank ordering of items remains stable even at the highest noise level. I need to verify what's already on those pages before cutting anything. The confounding explanation is earlier in the paper, so it won't help with the page count issue. Let me target the two sections that actually matter: the error propagation text after the table in §4.3 and the IRT discussion in Related Work. The closest antecedent, DG-IRT \\citep{shu2013dgirt}, is compared in Section~\\ref{sec:model_spec}.",
    "newString": "\\citet{jiao2025mfrm_llm} apply MFRM to LLM raters; \\citet{uto2020generalized}, \\citet{choi2026irt_judge}, and PSN-IRT \\citep{zhou2025psnirt} extend IRT/MFRM but none include contamination facets. DG-IRT \\citep{shu2013dgirt} is compared in Section~\\ref{sec:model_spec}.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Judge Bias in LLM Evaluation.}\nCyclicJudge \\citep{cyclicjudge2026} eliminates average bias via round-robin assignment but treats residual variation as genuine ability. UDA \\citep{uda2026} learns a neural K-factor reducing inter-judge deviation by 63.4\\% without modeling contamination. \\citet{fiedler2026bias_uncertainty} formalize judge variability via $J/\\Delta J$ diagnostics; \\citet{ye2024justice} and \\citet{panickssery2024selffavor} document position, verbosity, and self-favoritism biases. All model only the judge facet, absorbing contamination into abilities (Section~\\ref{sec:confounding}).\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination Detection and Avoidance.}\nConStat \\citep{constat2024}, DCR \\citep{dcr2025}, LogProber \\citep{logprober2024}, and Min-K\\% \\citep{mink2024} detect contamination but assume a fixed rater; \\citet{preference_leakage2026} identify judge-side leakage, complementary to G-MFRM's examinee-side focus. LiveBench \\citep{livebench2025}, TRUCE \\citep{truce2024}, and tinyBenchmarks \\citep{polo2024tinybenchmarks} prevent contamination by design; G-MFRM corrects for residual contamination in existing benchmarks.\n\n\\paragraph{IRT and MFRM for LLM Evaluation.}\n\\citet{jiao2025mfrm_llm} apply MFRM to LLM raters without contamination terms; \\citet{uto2020generalized}, \\citet{choi2026irt_judge}, and PSN-IRT \\citep{zhou2025psnirt} extend IRT/MFRM along other axes but none include contamination facets. The closest antecedent, DG-IRT \\citep{shu2013dgirt}, is compared in Section~\\ref{sec:model_spec}.",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VDJ3kuh9hFY8Xfcowt3qBe`</summary>

```text
50	The MCMC sampler achieves rank-normalized split-$\hat{R} \leq 1.03$ across all parameters with zero divergent transitions and minimum bulk ESS of 174 (217 for model-level parameters $\theta_n$, $c_n$, $d_j$). These values satisfy the traditional convergence thresholds ($\hat{R} < 1.05$, ESS $> 100$; \citealp{gelman2013bda}) but do not meet the stricter criteria of \citet{vehtari2021rhat} ($\hat{R} < 1.01$, ESS $> 400$), which may contribute to posterior overconfidence (synthetic CI coverage 71\%; see Limitations); we report point estimates and rank orderings as primary results. Model and judge facets show acceptable fit (all infit/outfit MNSQ within $[0.63, 1.07]$; item-level diagnostics in Appendix). Posterior predictive checks confirm adequate model fit (predicted response rate $0.593$ vs.\ observed $0.599$; Appendix~\ref{app:ppc}). Contamination susceptibility $\hat{c}_n$ exhibits a gradient by model age, directionally consistent with known training data exposure. Estimated $\hat{c}_n$ exceeds 1.0 for the four oldest models: GPT-3.5-Turbo ($\hat{c}_n = 5.60$), GPT-4-0613 ($4.20$), Qwen2.5-72B ($3.00$), and LLaMA-3.3-70B ($2.50$). The most recent models show low estimated contamination: GPT-4o ($0.88$) and GPT-4o-mini ($0.93$). The global shrinkage scale $\hat{\sigma}_c = 2.40$ confirms substantial inter-model heterogeneity ($\bar{c} = 2.50$, aligning with the strong-contamination regime in synthetic validation). Notably, sequential estimation yields near-zero contamination estimates ($\bar{c} \approx 0$), demonstrating that without joint modeling, contamination effects are entirely absorbed into ability estimates $\hat{\theta}$---precisely the omitted variable bias predicted by the theoretical analysis (Section~\ref{sec:confounding}). A per-model diagnostic (Table~\ref{tab:bias_absorption}) reveals that MFRM-only inflates ability estimates for the most contaminated models by up to 0.79 logits (GPT-3.5-Turbo), while deflating estimates for the least contaminated models by up to 0.40 logits (GPT-4o). Judge severity $d_j$ separates by provenance: proprietary judges tend to score more strictly (e.g., Claude Opus $+0.60$, GPT-5.5 $+0.53$) than open-source judges (e.g., Mistral $-0.74$, LLaMA $-0.42$); full $d_j$ values for all nine judges are reported in Table~\ref{tab:judge_severity}. Results are robust to prior specification (Horseshoe vs.\ HalfNormal: Pearson $r > 0.999$; Appendix) and to judge panel size: G-MFRM outperforms baselines at all judge counts $k \geq 2$, with $\tau$ increasing monotonically from $0.771$ ($k = 2$) to $0.857$ ($k = 9$); $k \geq 4$ suffices to exceed the full-panel baseline (Appendix~\ref{app:judge_count}).
51	
52	\subsection{Error Propagation Robustness}
```

[Director 指令] N=17 预写作（不依赖 judge 评测结果，现在就能做）

N=17 扩展正在跑 judge 评测。以下论文修改不依赖结果数据，请现在就完成：

## 1. §4.1 Setup 更新 judge 描述
原文说 "Nine LLM judges score all model responses"。N=17 扩展后设计变为 asymmetric：
- 原 8 模型：9 judges 全覆盖
- 新增 9 模型：7 judges（去掉 GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.7）

需要更新 §4.1 Setup 的描述，说明：
- 17 target models（8 original + 9 additional）
- 9 judges 中 7 judges 覆盖全部 17 模型，2 judges 仅覆盖原 8 模型
- 这是 connected incomplete design，Theorem 1 只要求连通性
- fill rate 会从 44.7% 进一步下降（具体数字等数据出来后填，先留占位符 XX%）

## 2. §4.1 target models 列表
原来只列 8 模型。扩展为 17 模型列表。可以分两组呈现或按 ELO 排序。新增模型：
claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022, gpt-4-turbo-2024-04-09, llama-3.1-70b-instruct, gemma-2-27b-it, claude-3-opus-20240229, llama-3-70b-instruct, mixtral-8x22b-instruct-v0.1, claude-3-haiku-20240307, llama-3-8b-instruct

## 3. Limitations 中 fill rate 讨论
第一个 limitation 提到 44.7% fill rate。这个数字会变——先留占位符，等数据出来更新。

## 4. Table 1 主结果表结构准备
Table 1 目前是 8 模型。需要扩展为 17 模型。可以先准备表结构（模型名列），数字用 TBD 占位。

## 约束
- 数字用 TBD/XX% 占位（等 MCMC 结果后统一填入）
- 正文 ≤ 8 页
- 不要删已有内容，只扩展

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01T9brhbFWXyCyZohRchjB2Y`</summary>

```text
1	We identify six limitations. First, although Theorem~\ref{thm:sufficient} requires only a connected design, the current evaluation achieves 44.7\% fill rate (5{,}661 of 12{,}672 potential cells); sparser designs may compromise identifiability of $c_n$ for models with few observed exposed items. Relatedly, sparse observations per item produce elevated item-level mean-square residuals (92/176 items), though model- and judge-level fit remains acceptable. Second, Stage~2 exposure estimation relies on a reference model anchor (gpt-4-0314), and exposure estimate quality depends on the anchor remaining uncontaminated relative to the benchmark; as benchmarks age, anchor validity should be periodically re-assessed. Third, the current evaluation uses a single benchmark (Arena-Hard) with eight target models; post-hoc power analysis indicates that detecting $\Delta\tau = 0.071$ at $\alpha = 0.05$ requires $N \approx 17$ models for 80\% power, while $N = 8$ yields approximately 50\% power, explaining the non-significant bootstrap result. Generalization to other benchmarks and larger model populations remains to be verified. Fourth, empirical CI coverage differs by parameter type: ability estimates $\theta$ approach nominal coverage at moderate sample sizes (${\sim}$90\% at $N = 8$), while contamination susceptibility $c_n$ exhibits moderate posterior overconfidence (71\% at $N = 50$, 68\% at $N = 8$; \citealp{talts2018sbc}). The $c_n$ under-coverage reflects the multiplicative coupling $c_n \cdot e_i$ and the HalfNormal prior's boundary constraint at $c_n = 0$, which systematically narrows posterior credible intervals. Crucially, this under-coverage does not improve with sample size (68\% at $N = 8$, 71\% at $N = 50$), indicating a structural rather than finite-sample issue. Point estimates and rank orderings ($\tau = 0.857$) are unaffected; $c_n$ credible intervals should be treated as lower bounds on uncertainty, with point estimates and relative ordering serving as the primary diagnostics. SBC-based posterior recalibration \citep{talts2018sbc} and explicit modeling of the asymmetric $c_n$ posterior are directions for future work. Fifth, while self-evaluation sensitivity analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:self_eval}) confirms robust rankings when excluding self-judging observations, a more comprehensive analysis removing overlapping models entirely from the judge panel has not been conducted. Sixth, the bilinear contamination term $c_n \cdot e_i$ assumes rank-1 structure---a single latent dimension of contamination susceptibility per model. In practice, contamination may be domain-specific (e.g., coding vs.\ reasoning items), requiring a higher-rank decomposition $\sum_r c_{nr} \cdot e_{ir}$ that the current model does not capture.
2	
3	Code and data will be released upon acceptance at [anonymized URL].
4
